## CORRECTION to the T3.00 entry above (coding-5, 2026-10-02)

The T3.00 entry says the fix "closes" G2-C1 / SE-2 and that the 256 KB cap plus the post-open info check bound the resource cost. **That was wrong** (G3-C1, found by review-20): the cap bounds metadata *bytes*, but Pillow's IFD reader allocates *bytes x IFD entry count*, and I never bounded the entry count. A 250 KB carrier with 4,000 entries loaded at a 978 MB tracemalloc peak and 10,000 entries reached 2.5 GB, in PNG eXIf, both JPEG paths and HEIF; and the JPEG pre-scan stopped at the first segment whose declared length was under 2 (`\xff\xe2\x00\x00`), which hid megabytes of Exif segments from it. What I got wrong: I sized every test shape to exceed the cap and measured "amplification without the guard" only for those shapes, so the evidence proved the cap worked for over-cap files and said nothing about a compliant file with many entries; and I copied Pillow's tolerance for the marker walk only where I had tested it, treating a short length as "stop" instead of asking what Pillow does after it. The T3.00 text above is left as written.

## FG3.1 Pillow never parses upload EXIF: G3-C1 and G3-W3 (coding-5, 2026-10-02; B-44) - tier: T1

**Run:** `cd /Users/calebschwarz/Documents/Hunting && .venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_image_io.py -q` -> exit 0, `349 passed`. Full suite `.venv/bin/pytest -q` -> exit 0, `4306 passed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed` (19 minutes; the machine was loaded). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean on both files; `bandit -c pyproject.toml` on `io.py` exit 0. I touched only `src/rga/vision/io.py`, `tests/unit/test_image_io.py` and this file; no other module referenced any removed private name.
- **Design (io.py):** (1) For JPEG and PNG the carriers are cut out of the raw bytes before `Image.open`: `_strip_jpeg` walks the markers as Pillow does up to the start of scan and removes APP1 `Exif` and XMP segments; `_strip_png` removes the `eXIf` chunk and the `Raw profile type exif`, `XML:com.adobe.xmp` and `xmp` text chunks (tEXt, bounded-inflated zTXt/iTXt), leaving every other chunk and segment byte for byte. Carrier bytes are charged against the 256 KB cap as they are found, so an oversize carrier is rejected before it is copied. (2) The orientation comes only from our bounded `_ifd0_orientation` (last SHORT, count-1 Orientation entry wins, as Pillow did; at most `MAX_IFD_ENTRIES = 256` entries are read; out-of-line values are never read), with an XMP `tiff:Orientation` regex fallback on the stripped XMP packets; the EXIF bytes are the JPEG APP1 payloads, the PNG eXIf chunk (preferred) or the decoded raw profile, or, for HEIF, the raw `info["exif"]` that pillow-heif already materialised. (3) No `getexif()`, `_getexif()`, `Exif.load` or IFD `load` call is reachable for uploads (a spy proves it for every container). (4) A JPEG segment with a declared length under 2 raises `ImageRejected("malformed JPEG segment length")` instead of ending the walk. (5) `Image.open` and `img.load()` map any `Exception` to `ImageRejected("image could not be decoded: <Type>")` (G3-W3: an empty `iCCP` after IDAT is an `IndexError`). (6) The byte cap stays as defense in depth, and HEIF additionally checks `info` carriers against it.
- **Behaviour kept:** a non-TIFF EXIF header (bad byte order, bad magic, under 8 bytes) is still `ImageRejected("invalid EXIF metadata ...")` in every container including HEIF; non-hex raw-profile text is still `invalid EXIF metadata: ValueError`; a damaged IFD or an Orientation of another type/count/value just yields no rotation; a bad tag sorted before Orientation still recovers the rotation (the bounded reader never stops at an out-of-range offset). **Behaviour changed (deliberate):** a PNG `tEXt`/`zTXt` chunk keyworded `xmp` used to be rejected only because Pillow tripped on its own str-vs-bytes bug; it is now read as an XMP packet (and rotates if it carries an orientation). A JPEG without JFIF density with a non-TIFF EXIF header used to load unrotated because Pillow swallowed the error at open; it is now rejected like the density path.
- **Tests (349, was 221):** every flood shape under the cap (250 KB payload; 120 KB for the raw profile, whose hex text must stay under 256 KB) at 4,000 and 10,000 entries in PNG eXIf (before and after IDAT), PNG raw profile, JPEG multi-APP1 (with and without JFIF density) and HEIF: loads, peak < 64 MB, < 2 s, and no Pillow EXIF/IFD parse (spy on `Image.getexif`, `_reload_exif`, `Exif.load`, `Exif.load_from_fp`, `JpegImageFile._getexif`, `ImageFileDirectory_v2.load`; the spy is shown to fire on a real `getexif()` call), plus a spy sweep over ordinary, bad-header, XMP and plain uploads in every container; carriers are really absent from the bytes Pillow opens (`info` has no exif/xmp/raw-profile key while dpi, other text chunks and plain files pass through unchanged); length-0 and length-1 JPEG segments (APP2, COM, APP0) hiding 4 MB/300-entry carriers: `ImageRejected`, peak < 64 MB, < 2 s, also with no carrier, length 2 valid, not walked after the start of scan, truncated final segments and chunks are not carriers; empty and one-byte `iCCP`, short `gAMA`/`cHRM`, before and after IDAT, are `ImageRejected`, the message is `image could not be decoded: IndexError`, and every exception class injected into `Image.open` or the PNG `load` maps; all 8 orientations x 6 container variants (PNG eXIf both placements, raw profile, JPEG both paths, HEIF) rotate correctly; bad-tag-before-Orientation recovery for orientations 3, 6, 8 in five containers; JPEG XMP orientation, EXIF wins over XMP, element-form XMP; 10,000 junk entries after Orientation 6 still rotate; an Orientation at index 255 is found and at index 256 ignored; the IFD walk of a 10,000-entry carrier x 50 takes under 1 s; the real photos still load at their EXIF-oriented size (existing test). Scanner robustness: 1,500 corrupted JPEG/PNG containers only return or raise `ImageRejected`.
- **Mutation run on a scratch copy** (`/tmp/coding5_scratch`, `-B`; deleted afterward; repo files never edited): 44 mutants, **44 KILLED** after one round of fixes. Killed: stripping not used for JPEG or PNG, Pillow `getexif` put back, length check `< 1` / break / removed, junk bytes not skipped, 0xFF padding unhandled, no stop at SOS, EXIF segment or chunk not cut, XMP not collected, prefix kept, charge `>=`, cap 128 KB / 512 KB, PNG eXIf and text not charged, the three PNG keywords dropped or roles swapped, inflate unbounded, raw-profile line offset, `fromhex` error unmapped, raw profile preferred over eXIf, no stop at IEND, IFD cap removed / 1000 / 128, first-entry-wins, orientation kind or count accepted loosely, byte order fixed, TIFF header or magic unchecked, XMP fallback removed or made to win, decode and open catches narrowed, HEIF info backstop removed, HEIF carriers ignored, aspect check removed. Three first-round survivors: truncated JPEG segment and truncated PNG chunk handling (added direct strip tests), and a dead `type(orientation) is not int` check (removed as unreachable). Not covered: a real iPhone HEIC (none available offline); pillow-heif's `info["exif"]` is read as raw EXIF with or without the `Exif\0\0` prefix, and a 4-byte offset prefix would give no rotation, as it did before this change.

## FG3.6 Group 3 fix wave: G3-W5, G3-W7, finalize aim (B-44, B-39 amended) (coding-6, 2026-10-02) - tier: T2 + real browser

**Run:** `cd /Users/calebschwarz/Documents/Hunting && .venv/bin/pytest tests/integration/test_api_review.py tests/integration/test_service_groups.py -q` -> exit **0**, `277 passed`. `ruff check src tests tools` clean, `ruff format --check` clean on the four touched Python files, `bandit -q -c pyproject.toml` on `services/groups.py` and `web/routes/review.py` exit 0, `node --check review.js` passes. `services/review.py` needed no change.
- **Semantics (`services/groups.py`):** an unmoved estimated shot is a placeholder and can be dropped or replaced with no rejection; detected, split, manual and user-moved shots (including moved estimated ones) still need one new `not_a_bullet_hole` rejection each. The silent-drop guard now counts only protected shots (stored vs. new), so a placeholder cannot stand in for a dropped detected shot. Rejections are matched as a multiset: each incoming rejection consumes at most one unmatched stored rejection at the same point, any further one is new, so a repeated PUT stays idempotent while two co-located shots can both be removed (two rejections at one point).
- **Editor (`review.js`):** Fill shortfall and Detect replace only unmoved estimated shots and keep every other shot (proposal shots within one bullet diameter of a kept shot are dropped as duplicates); deleting an unmoved estimated shot records no rejection; when the count exceeds the expected count Finalize is disabled with "N of M shots: remove K with Not a bullet hole, or raise the expected count." (and a shortfall says how many to add), so the flow never dead-ends on 409.
- **Finalize response (`routes/review.py`):** `POST finalize` nulls `aim` for a masked group (and the stored aim is untouched); the `PUT review` response keeps it for the editor.
- **Tests added (service, 11 groups of cases):** placeholder dropped without a rejection; two co-located estimated shots both removable; placeholders replaced and the count grown or shrunk; detected, manual, split, moved-estimated and moved-detected still refused without a rejection (accepted with one); a placeholder cannot replace a dropped detected shot; a mixed drop needs one rejection for the protected shot only; two co-located detected shots need two rejections and a repeat stays at two; a stored rejection does not pay for a later co-located drop; a finalize after dropping a placeholder. Two older tests that encoded the coordinate-dedupe were rewritten to per-shot semantics (a repeated save is idempotent, a second rejection at the same point is recorded).
- **Tests added (API, `ragged_10x7`, review-21's flow):** save 10, lower the expected count to 9, Fill shortfall, apply the page's merge, Save and Finalize all return 200 with 0 rejections and 2 estimated shots left; a moved estimated shot kept over the expected count saves with 200, Finalize is a 409 `count_mismatch`, dropping it without a reason is a 409 `shot_dropped_without_reason`, with its rejection 200, then Finalize 200; co-located estimated shots removable; co-located detected shots need one rejection each; a detected shot still needs its reason; finalize aim null for a masked (`zero_check`) group while its PUT response and database keep the aim, kept for an unmasked group, still null on a repeated finalize; script checks for `isPlaceholder`, the merge filter and the over-count reason text.
- **Real browser (Chromium, real server, marked `e2e`):** (1) Save, set expected 9, Fill shortfall -> "9 of 9 shots", Finalize enabled, Save says "Saved.", Finalize navigates to the group page; the database has a final group with 9 shots (2 estimated) and no console errors. (2) Drag an estimated shot far (moved), Save, expected 9, Fill -> "10 of 9 shots", Finalize disabled with "remove 1 with Not a bullet hole, or raise the expected count", Save still says "Saved."; select the moved shot and press Not a bullet hole -> "9 of 9 shots", Finalize enabled, Save and Finalize succeed, exactly one rejection stored. ## CORRECTION to the FG3.1 entry above (coding-5, 2026-10-02)

The FG3.1 entry says "No `getexif()`, `_getexif()`, `Exif.load` or IFD `load` call is reachable for uploads (a spy proves it for every container)". **That was wrong for JPEG.** The claim rested on a spy over my own test inputs, not over the space of marker sequences Pillow accepts. `_strip_jpeg` treated 0xC8 and 0xF0-0xFD as length-bearing and skipped the segment they declare, but Pillow's marker table gives them no handler (no length), so an `FF F0 <len>` wrapper over an APP1 Exif segment hid the carrier from my walk while Pillow scanned into it, found the APP1 and loaded its IFD on open (G3c2-C1); and an APP2 `MPF` segment was neither stripped nor charged, so `jpeg_factory` handed it to `_getmp` and the IFD loader (G3c2-W1, about 336 MB from 64 KB). What I got wrong: I wrote my walk from my own picture of JPEG framing and tested it against shapes I had already thought of, instead of reading `JpegImagePlugin.MARKER` and mirroring it, and I had no generic guard that would fail on a sequence I had not imagined. The FG3.1 text above is left as written.

## FG3b.1 whitelist rebuilding: G3c2-C1 and G3c2-W1 (coding-5, 2026-10-02; B-50) - tier: T1

**Run:** `cd /Users/calebschwarz/Documents/Hunting && .venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_image_io.py -q` -> exit 0, `470 passed` (+ the real-photo test, which runs here). Full suite `.venv/bin/pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> exit 0, `4634 passed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed` (17.5 minutes). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean on both files; `bandit -c pyproject.toml` on `io.py` exit 0. I touched only `src/rga/vision/io.py`, `tests/unit/test_image_io.py` and this file; no process was stopped, and PID 58042 and port 8765 were not touched.
- **Design (io.py):** `_strip_jpeg`/`_strip_png` became `_rebuild_jpeg`/`_rebuild_png`: the upload is re-emitted from a whitelist, so Pillow only ever sees bytes we chose. **JPEG:** read from `JpegImagePlugin.MARKER` on disk. Kept: SOI, DQT, DHT, DRI, the SOF0/1/2 frame headers Pillow decodes, APP0 starting `JFIF`, APP14 starting `Adobe`, APP2 `ICC_PROFILE` (up to `MAX_ICC_BYTES` = 1 MB in total, then dropped, not rejected), and everything from SOS to the end verbatim (entropy data, later scans, EOI; none of it is parsed by Pillow's header walk or by Python). Dropped: every other APPn (including APP1 Exif/XMP, whose payloads are kept aside, charged against the 256 KB cap, for our orientation reader, and APP2 `MPF`) and COM; junk bytes and 0xFF fill between segments are dropped as Pillow skips them. Rejected as `ImageRejected`: a declared length under 2, any marker without a length (0x00, 0x01, 0x02-0xBF unknown, 0xC8, RSTn, SOI, EOI, JPGn 0xF0-0xFD), unsupported frames and tables (SOF3, 5-7, 9-15, DAC, DNL, DHP, EXP), a segment running past the end, and a file with no scan. **PNG:** kept IHDR, PLTE, IDAT, IEND, tRNS, gAMA, cHRM, sRGB, pHYs, sBIT and an iCCP of up to 1 MB; every other chunk (all text chunks, eXIf, tIME, bKGD, sPLT, animation chunks, private chunks) is dropped, with eXIf and the raw-profile and XMP text kept aside for the orientation reader; data after IEND is dropped; a chunk past the end or a missing IEND is rejected. HEIF is unchanged (raw `info["exif"]` into our reader). Pillow is therefore never shown an APP1, an MPF, an eXIf or a text chunk.
- **Tests (470, was 349):** (1) the two reproducers: `FF F0 <len>` wrapping an APP1 Exif segment, for 0xC8 and every 0xF0-0xFD, is `ImageRejected` with peak < 64 MB and no IFD load; a 64 KB APP2 `MPF` with 5,000 entries (and five of them beside an Exif segment) loads, peak < 64 MB, no IFD load, orientation from the Exif still applied. (2) A generic guard, `ifd_load_spy`, over `Image.getexif`, `Exif.load`, `Exif.load_from_fp`, `JpegImageFile._getexif` and `ImageFileDirectory_v2.load` (which `_getmp` calls), and a second test showing the spy does fire when Pillow is allowed to parse an Exif or MPF carrier. (3) **Marker-sequence fuzz:** 3,000 deterministic JPEGs with random placements of valid and flood-sized Exif, XMP, MPF (valid and flood), ICC, random APPn, COM, JPGn markers with and without following bytes, JPGn wrappers over Exif with true and false lengths, RSTn, 0xFF padding, junk bytes, length-0/1 segments, DNL/DHP/EXP/DAC and Adobe; every case either loads or raises `ImageRejected`, the IFD spy never fires, and the worst tracemalloc peak stays < 64 MB (at least 300 loaded and 300 rejected, so it is not vacuous). **PNG chunk-sequence fuzz:** 1,500 cases with random eXIf, raw-profile, XMP, text, tIME, private, iCCP, gAMA and pHYs chunks before and after IDAT, same assertions. (4) Whitelist behavior: each dropped JPEG segment kind (12 kinds) leaves the rebuilt bytes equal to the plain JPEG and the decoded pixels identical; kept parts survive byte for byte (JFIF, ICC, Adobe); baseline, progressive and optimized files rebuild identically and load; grayscale and CMYK load; junk and fill bytes are dropped; ICC beyond the cap dropped not rejected; ~50 ambiguous or unsupported markers rejected in three framings each; no scan, truncated header, truncated segment; data after the scan is passed through unparsed; the rebuilt pixels equal Pillow's own decode of the original. PNG: ten dropped chunk kinds before and after IDAT, five kept kinds byte for byte, palette plus tRNS pixels, iCCP at and over the cap, trailing data, missing IEND, truncated chunk, a bad-CRC dropped chunk never reaching Pillow. The existing tests pass except seven whose expectations changed because the whitelist drops what stripping used to leave: `Comment` text chunks are now dropped, data after IEND is dropped, truncated carriers are now rejected instead of left to Pillow, and a JPEG with no scan or a cut header raises from the rebuild; they were updated (stated in the test names). All 8 orientations x 6 containers, bad-tag-before-Orientation recovery, flood shapes at 4,000 and 10,000 entries (peak < 64 MB, < 2 s) and the real photos pass unchanged.
- **Mutation run on a scratch copy** (`/tmp/coding5_scratch`, `-B`; deleted afterward; repo files never edited): 43 mutants plus one re-run, **40 killed, 4 equivalent survivors, then 1 more killed after a new test** (net: all non-equivalent mutants killed). ## CORRECTION to the FG3b.1 entry above (coding-5, 2026-10-02)

The FG3b.1 entry says the generic IFD-load spy and the marker/chunk fuzz "guard" the whitelist and that peaks stay under 64 MB. **Two of those claims were not backed by the tests** (G3c3-C1, G3c3-W5). (1) The fuzz never generated many kept or dropped segments, so "peak < 64 MB" held only for its own inputs: the rebuild kept one Python object per kept segment or chunk with no limit, and a 40 MB JPEG of empty DHT segments (about 10.5 million) loaded, took about 20 s and grew the server's RSS by about 1.5 GB; the FG3b.1 rebuild was a regression from the FG3.1 code on that input. (2) For PNG the spy assertion could not fail: Pillow's PNG open and load never call the spied loaders on their own, so the 1,500-case PNG fuzz passed with the rebuild bypassed, and a bypass mutant passed all 470 tests. What I got wrong: I treated "the output is a whitelist" as if it bounded the work, never asking how many items a whitelisted input may contain, and I wrote a guard whose pass condition held with or without the control it was meant to prove, without ever running it against an unrebuilt control. The FG3b.1 text above is left as written.

## FG3c.1 bounded rebuild and load-path proof: G3c3-C1 and G3c3-W5 (coding-5, 2026-10-02; B-55) - tier: T1

**Run:** `cd /Users/calebschwarz/Documents/Hunting && .venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_image_io.py -q` -> exit 0, `532 passed` (+ the real-photo test). Full suite `.venv/bin/pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> exit 0, `4838 passed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed` (19.8 minutes; the machine was loaded). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean; `bandit -c pyproject.toml` on `io.py` exit 0; every public name has a docstring. I touched only `src/rga/vision/io.py`, `tests/unit/test_image_io.py` and this file. The real photos are read in place by the existing test only; no copy of them was written (B-54).
- **Fix, JPEG (`_rebuild_jpeg`):** the output is now a few `memoryview` slices of the input joined once, or the input object itself when nothing is dropped (no copy at all); never one object per segment. Caps, each `ImageRejected("too many image segments")`: at most `MAX_JPEG_SEGMENTS` = 1,024 segments walked before SOS (kept or dropped, so a flood of dropped COM or APP segments is bounded too), `MAX_KEPT_SEGMENTS` = 64 kept non-scan segments (ICC counted), `MAX_KEPT_HEADER_BYTES` = 1 MB of kept header bytes outside SOS (ICC has its own 1 MB budget and is not charged here), and `MAX_JUNK_BYTES` = 4,096 bytes of junk or 0xFF fill between segments (junk is skipped with `bytes.find`, so a 40 MB junk run is rejected at C speed). Also tightened, because Pillow does per-byte work on them: one SOF only and at most 64 payload bytes (Pillow adds one tuple per 3 payload bytes), and a DRI must be exactly 4 bytes long. Dropped runs are coalesced into one slice.
- **Fix, PNG (`_rebuild_png`):** at most `MAX_PNG_CHUNKS` = 20,000 chunks walked (IDAT included, so a 40 MB file needs an average IDAT of 2 KB or more; encoders write 8 KB to 64 KB) and `MAX_KEPT_SEGMENTS` = 64 kept non-IDAT chunks; kept chunks must have a possible size (IHDR 13, gAMA 4, cHRM 32, sRGB 1, pHYs 9, sBIT 1-4, PLTE 3-768, tRNS 0-256, else `malformed PNG chunk size`; the review noted Pillow unpacks `len // 4` values from a cHRM of any length); all iCCP chunks share one 1 MB budget; runs of kept chunks (IDAT included) and runs of dropped chunks each cost one slice, and the output is joined once or is the input itself. Short fixed-size chunks (gAMA, cHRM, pHYs) are now rejected by size before Pillow's loader instead of raising `struct.error` there; the mapping of `struct.error` from the decoder is still tested by injection.
- **Measured on the review's shapes (40 MB files, tracemalloc peak, time):** empty DHT, empty DQT, DRI, JFIF, Adobe, tiny ICC, empty COM, empty Exif, empty XMP, other APP1, APP15, 0x00 junk, 0xFF fill JPEGs: all `ImageRejected("too many image segments")` in under 0.02 s at 0.0-0.2 MB (was +1.5 GB and 20 s for DHT). sBIT, pHYs, sRGB, tIME, tEXt, xmp tEXt, private, empty IDAT and tiny iCCP PNG floods: all rejected in 0.0-0.2 s at 0.0 MB (the first version of the PNG cap, 100,000 chunks, took 1 s and 18 MB for dropped-chunk floods, which led to the 20,000 cap and the run coalescing). A child-process test measures `ru_maxrss` before and after the 40 MB empty-DHT JPEG and the 40 MB sBIT PNG: growth under 64 MB and under 2 s.
- **Tests (532, was 470):** all of the above as separate tests (13 JPEG and 9 PNG flood kinds at the upload cap; two child-process RSS tests); exact boundaries for every cap and every PNG chunk size (64 kept loads and 65 rejects, 1,024 walked loads and 1,025 rejects, junk 4,096 loads and 4,097 rejects, 20,000 PNG chunks loads and 20,001 rejects, kept-byte cap with 15 versus 18 segments of 60 KB, ICC outside the byte budget, SOF duplicate or oversize, DRI size, shared iCCP budget); a legitimate PNG with the IDAT stream split into thousands of chunks loads with identical pixels and a small peak; a 40 MB JPEG and a 40 MB PNG with one dropped part cost one copy (peak below input + 64 MB); input with nothing to drop is returned as the same object with under 1 MB peak; a run of 19,995 dropped chunks peaks under 256 KB. **G3c3-W5:** a spy on `Image.open` shows the bytes Pillow is handed equal the rebuilt bytes and contain none of the dropped chunks or segments, for PNG and JPEG, and that HEIF is opened unmodified; a dropped PNG chunk with a bad CRC, and PNGs whose dropped acTL/fcTL/fdAT chunk makes Pillow alone raise `ValueError`, load through `load_oriented` (a bypass cannot pass these); the fuzz tests now end with an unrebuilt control (60 eXIf PNGs and 60 Exif JPEGs opened straight through Pillow must fire the spy; at least 20 hits asserted) and three standalone tests show 60 of 60 unrebuilt controls fire it while the same inputs through `load_oriented` never do.
- **Existing tests changed (with reason):** the five tests that expected short gAMA, cHRM and pHYs chunks to fail inside Pillow now expect `malformed PNG chunk size` (the size check runs first), and the post-IDAT undecodable cases keep only the empty and one-byte iCCP shapes (a not-zlib iCCP loads in Pillow); the old explicit `struct.error` test became an injection test.
- **Mutation run on a scratch copy** (`/tmp/coding5_scratch`, `-B`; deleted afterward; repo files never edited): 33 mutants plus two re-runs, **all killed after one round of new tests; 2 first-round survivors fixed by tests** (ICC wrongly counted in the kept-byte budget: killed by a case that exceeds the budget only if ICC is counted; per-chunk instead of per-run slices for dropped PNG chunks: killed by the 256 KB peak test). Killed include: JPEG walk cap removed or off by one, kept-segment cap removed or 63 or 65, kept-byte cap removed or 2 MB or 512 KB, junk cap removed (junk and fill) or off by one, junk kept, second SOF allowed, SOF size unchecked, DRI size unchecked, ICC uncapped, rebuild always copying; PNG walk cap removed or off by one or 100,000, kept cap removed or counting IDAT, chunk sizes unchecked (all, upper only, lower only), iCCP budget removed, always copying, data after IEND kept; **PNG bypass** (open the original bytes for PNG) and JPEG bypass and "always open `data`". The unbounded-walk mutant took 269 s to be killed because the flood tests then ran for real, which is itself the proof the caps are load-bearing.
- Not covered: a 20,000-chunk legitimate PNG was assumed (not found in the wild) to be absurd for a 40 MB phone photo; a JPEG with more than 1,024 header segments or more than 4 KB of junk, or a PNG with more than 64 non-IDAT kept chunks, is now rejected; a real iPhone HEIC; SE-2 still needs a Group 3 cycle to verify.

No JS file except `api.js` may call `fetch(`.

- Fail loudly: a path, method or body the extractor cannot read, a spread or computed key in a body, an API function used as a value, and a new direct `fetch(` raise or fail the test (unit tests for each).
- Verify the verifier (on scratch copies of the JS, tmp dirs, the repo JS untouched): each of these planted defects is reported: renamed paths (check-groups, detach, aggregates list, sessions, lots), wrong methods (PATCH to PUT, DELETE to POST), a renamed body key (also nested), a dropped required key, a body sent to a body-less route, renamed query parameters (sessions, groups list), and missing response fields (`aim_candidate`, `group.mask`, a field read through a function parameter, through a variable assigned in another function, on saved aggregates, on the zeroing group list); a route registered in `ROUTE_SCHEMAS` but missing in the app; a body whose keys are valid but that the model rejects (`candidate_index` together with `manual`); an unreadable call; a direct `fetch`; an API function used as a value. The unplanted copy passes.

The first run had 3 survivors (route-not-in-app, loops, destructuring); each got its own test.

## T4.0b progressive-JPEG scan cap: SE-3 (coding-5, 2026-10-02; B-57/B-58) - tier: T1

**Run:** `cd /Users/calebschwarz/Documents/Hunting && .venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_image_io.py -q` -> exit 0, `571 passed` (+ the real-photo test, which runs here). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean; `bandit -c pyproject.toml` on `io.py` exit 0. Full suite `.venv/bin/pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> `4943 passed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed, 1 failed` (26.8 minutes, machine loaded). **The one failure is not from this change:** `tests/integration/test_repo_zeroing.py::TestDataSchemaContract::test_the_module_defines_sql_and_all_of_it_is_constant_text` asserts exactly 10 SQL literals in `src/rga/db/repo_zeroing.py`, and another worker added an eleventh (`_LIST_ZEROS_FOR_RIFLE`, file mtime 07:59) without updating that test (it also needs the new statement covered by the EXPLAIN and index-plan checks). It passes in every other respect (113 of 114). I touched only `io.py`, `test_image_io.py` and this file; PID 7967 was not touched and no photo copy was written.
- **Reproduced first:** a flat 2000x1500 progressive JPEG (18 KB, 10 scans) whose final 15-byte scan is repeated: raw `Image.open().load()` takes 0.47 s for 100 repeats and 4.0 s for 1,000 (review-32: 36.8 s for 10,000 under load). After the fix the 10,000-repeat file (168 KB) is rejected in under 0.01 s. My first attempt at a flood, with hand-made scan headers, was *not* a CPU pin: libjpeg rejected it immediately, which is why the tests use the repeated real last scan instead.
- **Fix (`io.py`):** `_check_jpeg_scans(data, pos)`, called from `_rebuild_jpeg` at the first SOS, counts scans in the entropy-coded data and raises `ImageRejected("too many progressive scans")` above `MAX_JPEG_SCANS` = 64 (the first scan counts). It walks the data at C speed with one regular expression, `FF` followed by anything except 00 (a stuffed byte), D0-D7 (restart markers) and FF (fill), so those are never mistaken for markers and a 40 MB run of stuffed bytes costs well under a second; marker segments between scans are skipped by their declared length (an `FF DA` inside a DHT payload is not counted); the walk ends at EOI (scans after it are never decoded); markers without a length are skipped as standalone; a declared length under 2 is rejected as before; and the number of markers walked is bounded by `MAX_JPEG_SEGMENTS`. Typical progressive files have 10 scans; the cap is far above that.
- **Tests (571, was 532):** Pillow-generated progressive files load with pixels identical to Pillow's own decode and are passed through unchanged: RGB, gray, quality 40 and 95 (1000x800), restart markers by rows and by blocks (so real RSTn bytes are in the data), 4:4:4, optimized, progressive CMYK; review-32's 10,000-scan file is rejected in under 0.5 s with a tracemalloc peak under 64 MB, before `Image.open` is called; a 40 MB file of repeated scans is rejected at once; 40 repeated scans are shown to decode fine in Pillow alone, so the cap is what stops the flood; **the boundary:** a file with exactly 64 scans loads end to end and 65 is rejected (also on a noisy file through the rebuild alone); a baseline file counts as one scan; scans after EOI are not counted; a truncated progressive file is left to the decoder. The counting rules are tested directly: `FF 00 DA` x5,000 is data, 20,000 restart-marker groups cost nothing, `FF FF FF DA` still counts, scan headers inside arbitrary noise are found, `FF DA` inside another segment's payload is skipped, a segment between scans is skipped by length, the walk ends at EOI, the first scan is included in the count, a flood of 1,025 other markers is `too many image segments`, length-0/1 segments are rejected, standalone markers (0x01, 0x02, 0xC8, 0xF0, 0xFD, SOI) do not swallow scans that follow, a segment past the end ends the walk, and 40 MB of `FF 00` is walked in under 1 s. Existing tests (including all 8 orientations, the floods and the real photos) pass unchanged.
- **Mutation run on a scratch copy** (`/tmp/coding5_scratch`, run with `-B`; deleted afterward; repo files never edited): 16 mutants plus one re-run, **all killed**, one after a new test. Killed: the check not called, the cap removed, `>=`, 128 and 32, the first scan not counted, stuffed `FF 00` treated as a marker, restart markers treated as markers, fill bytes hiding a marker, EOI not ending the walk, segment lengths ignored, the wrong marker counted as a scan, the marker cap removed, the length-under-2 check removed, the message changed, and standalone markers read as length-bearing (this survivor led to the new standalone-marker test).
- Not covered: a progressive JPEG with more than 64 legitimate scans would now be rejected (none seen from phones or Pillow); arithmetic-coded progressive files were already rejected as unsupported.

## T4.0f Settings tab holding the Offline/Online switch (B-60) (devops-1, 2026-10-02) - tier: T1 (templates) + T2 (page through the app)

## T4.0h repo_zeroing contract test for `_LIST_ZEROS_FOR_RIFLE` (coding-5, 2026-10-02) - tier: T2

**Run:** `cd /Users/calebschwarz/Documents/Hunting && .venv/bin/pytest tests/integration/test_repo_zeroing.py -q` -> exit 0, `126 passed` (was 114, with the one failure). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean. Test file only; `src/rga/db/repo_zeroing.py` was not touched. `test_layering` and `test_no_forbidden_metrics` still green.
- **The failure and its fix:** coding-4's new `_LIST_ZEROS_FOR_RIFLE` (and `list_zeros_for_rifle`, `ZeroListing`) made the AST scan find 11 SQL literals instead of 10; the count assertion is now 11. The existing EXPLAIN-every-statement test already covers the new statement, and new tests give it the same contract checks as the others.
- **Plan (reported as asked):** `SEARCH zeros USING INDEX ix_zeros_rifle_load (rifle_id=?)`, `SEARCH loads USING INTEGER PRIMARY KEY`, `SEARCH load_lots USING INTEGER PRIMARY KEY ... LEFT-JOIN`, and `USE TEMP B-TREE FOR ORDER BY`. There is no table scan; the temp b-tree is the `ORDER BY zeros.id DESC`, which cannot use the index (its key is `(rifle_id, load_id, state)`) and sorts only one rifle's zeros, so I left it as is and the test asserts no `SCAN` of zeros, loads or load_lots rather than forbidding the sort.
- **New tests (12):** the plan test above; the selected columns are exactly the `zeros` columns in table order plus `load_label` and `lot_code`, the row dataclass fields equal the table columns, and `ZeroListing` is `(zero, load_label, lot_code)`; behaviour of `list_zeros_for_rifle`: newest first with load label and lot code, each item carries the stored row unchanged, superseded and completed zeros are listed too, a zero with no lot survives the LEFT JOIN (lot code None), only the requested rifle's zeros appear, an empty rifle and an unknown rifle give an empty list, ordering follows the row id and not the creation timestamp, the label is the load's current label, the call is read-only and opens no transaction, and the zero row validates against `ZeroOut`.
- **Mutation run on a scratch copy** (`/tmp/coding5_scratch`, `-B`; deleted afterward; repo files never edited): 12 mutants, **12 killed**: rifle filter removed, ascending order, order by creation time, inner join instead of left join, label taken from the lot, lot code taken from the load, wrong load join, wrong lot join, explicit column list missing the star, label dropped in the function, wrong bound parameter, and the dataclass field order swapped.

## T4.10 AC-9 and T4.11 AC-10 e2e journeys (coding-1)

Files: `tests/e2e/test_ac9_ragged.py`, `tests/e2e/test_ac10_guards.py`; harness (`tests/e2e/pages.py`, coding-6's): new `review_set_region` helper and a race fix in `confirm_reference`. Tests only; no app code changed. PID 7967 and port 8765 untouched.

- `.venv/bin/pytest tests/e2e/test_ac9_ragged.py -q` -> exit 0, 1 passed. `.venv/bin/pytest tests/e2e/test_ac10_guards.py -q` -> exit 0, 4 passed. Together with the harness self-tests (`tests/e2e/test_e2e_harness.py`): `19 passed`, three runs in a row (about 110 s each); ruff clean.
- **AC-9** (`ragged_10x7`, .224 rifle): 10 of 10 shots; exactly 3 `circle.shot.estimated`, each centre inside the truth ragged outline (truth outline mapped plane -> image -> working px through the confirmed reference, `cv2.pointPolygonTest >= 0`); 3 "est" labels; the "includes estimated shots" badge is shown; dragging the outermost estimated shot 90 screen px away from the group raises the live extreme spread by more than 0.05 in and keeps 10 of 10 and the badge; after marking the aim, Save and Finalize, the group page shows n = 10 with the estimated badge.
- **AC-10, no reference** (`no_reference`): Find for "1 inch circle" and "1 inch grid" both return 0 proposals, the "Nothing was found automatically" note shows and "Use this proposal" is disabled; the review page shows "not confirmed" with Detect disabled; the API refuses detect with 409 `reference_unconfirmed` and no shots exist; a manual 4-click circle then confirms and the editor detects.
- **AC-10, delete control:** the only danger button is "Not a bullet hole" (no delete/remove/discard/clear button, no reason picker); using it takes 5 of 5 to 4 of 5 and stores one rejection with reason `not_a_bullet_hole`.
- **AC-10, finalize at 9/10:** with the aim set, 10 of 10 enables Finalize; deleting one shot gives 9 of 10, Finalize disabled with "add 1 missing shot(s)"; after Save the API refuses finalize with 409 `count_mismatch` and the group stays draft; clicking the removed spot restores 10 of 10, Finalize works.
- **AC-10, two groups on one photo** (`multi_dot_target`): both groups reuse the one stored photo (one photos row). Each is fenced by dragging its truth region in the editor; each ends with 5 shots, every one within 0.1 in of one of its own truth holes and more than 0.3 in from the other group's holes, different stored regions, and its own aim within 0.05 in of truth. T4.0a at each step: the suggestion panel says none selected, no ring is active, "Use this aim" is disabled and the DB aim is null; group 1 takes Next then Use, group 2 clicks the ring at its own aim then Use.
- **Verifier checks** on a scratch copy (`-B`, wrapper importing the scratch tree), all 7 mutants KILLED: estimated badge hidden; shot drag ignored; aim pre-selected; finalize count rule removed; delete button renamed; reference guard removed in the service; editor buttons never disabled.
- **App defects and notes for the lead (not fixed):** (1) The editor's automatic first detect runs over the whole photo when no region is stored; on a multi-aim photo it proposes every hole ("15 of 5"). Setting a region and pressing Detect adds nothing and removes nothing, because `mergeProposal` keeps every unsaved detected shot, so the wrong shots can only be cleared as "Not a bullet hole" (recording false rejections). The journey works around it: Detect stores the region, then reloading the review page re-runs the automatic detect inside it (5 of 5). (2) Not a defect but a trap: the default 6.5 Creedmoor bullet diameter (.264) makes the detector see the ragged hole as one hole (1 detected and 2 stacked estimated); the synthetic targets use .224, so the journeys create .224 rifles. (3) Harness race fixed: `confirm_reference` read `#reference-app` after clicking Continue and timed out when navigation finished first; it now waits for the review URL right after the click (tests/e2e/pages.py, coding-6 informed through this note).

## T4.0o (B-65) group share image renderer (coding-5)

Files: `src/rga/services/share.py`, `src/rga/web/routes/share.py` (new); `src/rga/web/schemas.py` (`ShareLayer`, `SHARE_DEFAULT_LAYERS`, `ShareQuery`, registry entry `("GET", "/api/groups/{id}/share.png")`); `tests/integration/test_share_image.py` (new, 60 tests); `tests/integration/test_id_bounds.py` (share route explicit-coverage test, two `OTHER_IDS` rows); `tests/unit/test_schemas.py` (`NO_RESPONSE_BODY` gains the share route, reason stated, like the photo image route).

Decisions: omitted layers are reported in the response header `X-Share-Omitted` (and drawn ones in `X-Share-Layers`), never on the card, so a shared image stays clean and the UI can tell the user. Default layers when `layers` is absent: `photo,spread` (the page's default-on layers); `layers=` is shots only. Aim mark and shots always drawn where the page draws them. `correction` also draws the POI mark, fades shots and hides numbers, as the page's correction view does. Suppressor label: "Suppressed (name)" with T4.0n landed, "Suppressed" with no name, "Unsuppressed". The header reads session fields only in `_conditions` (B-66: one accessor). Bounds: card width 1000 (cap `MAX_WIDTH` 2000), height cap 2600, working image over 36 MP is dropped, 2 concurrent renders (503 `share_busy`), 10 s budget (503 `share_timeout`), a locked DB is 503 `busy`; all with Retry-After 1. Unreadable/oversized/missing working image drops the photo layer and lists it as omitted (never a 500). No files written (B-54), no network.

## Fix: "every page template extends base" now exempts macro-only partials (devops-1, 2026-10-02) - tier: T1

**Cause:** `test_every_page_template_extends_base_and_only_settings_holds_the_mode_switch` failed on `suppressor_picker.html`, a Jinja macro partial that pages import and never render. Only `tests/unit/test_templates_shell.py` changed.

**Rule (structural, no name list):** a template is a macro-only partial when it has no `{% extends %}`, at least one balanced `{% macro %}...{% endmacro %}` block (nesting handled, whitespace-control variants accepted), and nothing at the top level besides macro blocks, Jinja comments, import tags and whitespace. Any markup, block, call or stray text outside the macros makes it a page, and a page must extend base.

**Run:** `.venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_templates_shell.py -q` -> 95 passed. The 6 other results are errors in `test_every_navigation_page_links_to_settings_and_has_no_mode_switch_in_its_header[...]` at fixture setup: `ImportError: cannot import name 'SessionCreate' from 'rga.web.schemas'`, the B-66 redesign window (R2 rewrote `schemas.py` before the routes were moved); with that test deselected the file is clean, and none of it comes from this change. `ruff check` clean.

**New tests:** 3 macro-only samples (single macro, comment plus import plus several macros with whitespace control, nested macros) are recognised; 10 other samples (a plain page, empty file, comment only, extends plus macro, markup after a macro, markup before a macro, a block next to a macro, a macro followed by a call, an unclosed macro, a stray endmacro) are not; a scratch directory with a base, a page and a partial is clean, and adding a standalone page and a macro file with stray markup reports exactly those two.

**Planted-fault check on a scratch copy (real tree untouched):** control with the real `suppressor_picker.html` passes; a page that does not extend base -> fails naming `standalone.html`; the macro file with stray markup appended -> fails; the macro file with a `{% block %}` appended -> fails; a new macro-only partial -> passes; the partial replaced by plain markup -> fails.

## R3 (SE-4) bounded EXIF capture-time reader, `vision/io.py` (coding-5)

Files: `src/rga/vision/io.py` (`LoadedImage`, `load_image`, `read_taken_at`, `_exif_taken_at` plus helpers `_tiff_of`, `_ifd_entries`, `_ascii_value`, `_taken_at_text`; `load_oriented` is now a thin wrapper with the same signature and errors); `tests/unit/test_image_io_taken_at.py` (new, 117 tests).

Contract: `taken_at` is local `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS` and `taken_at_offset` is `+HH:MM` (within 14:00), both `str | None`. One bounds-checked hop IFD0 -> 0x8769 (LONG, count 1; the first one counts) -> Exif sub-IFD, each walk capped at `MAX_IFD_ENTRIES`; 0x9003 else 0x9004 (ASCII, count 20, 19 chars plus NUL at an offset >= 8 and inside the data); 0x9011 (ASCII, count 7). The date must match `\d{4}:\d{2}:\d{2} \d{2}:\d{2}:\d{2}`, be a real datetime, and have a year from 1990 to next year. Any failure gives None for that value and never raises; a bad date never rejects an image. A sub-IFD pointer inside the TIFF header, outside the data, or back at IFD0 is not followed; no other pointer is. The TIFF is walked through a `memoryview`, so no copy of the EXIF is made. No XMP fallback. Pillow still never parses upload EXIF (the IFD spy stays silent). `read_taken_at(data)` returns `(None, None)` on any failure and decodes no pixels; it walks the same whitelisted JPEG/PNG carriers, and for HEIC opens the file lazily through pillow_heif for its raw EXIF bytes only.

Commands and results:
- `.venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_image_io_taken_at.py -q` -> exit 0, 117 passed. `.venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_image_io.py tests/unit/test_image_io_taken_at.py -q` -> 688 passed (the 571 existing io tests unchanged and green).
- `.venv/bin/ruff check src tests/unit` clean; `.venv/bin/bandit -c pyproject.toml -r src/rga/vision/io.py` clean.
- Coverage of the brief: tracemalloc on 10,000-entry sub-IFDs and IFD0s (peak under 100 kB, control: an uncapped reader allocates over 500 kB on the same input; the whole JPEG load path with a 10,000-entry flood loads with no date); entry-cap boundaries (entry 256 read, entry 257 not, in IFD0 and the sub-IFD); out-of-bounds, header, self and IFD0 pointers; a sub-IFD whose entries point back at itself or at IFD0; a date listed in IFD0 itself is not read when the pointer targets IFD0; wrong pointer or value type and count; missing NUL; value ending exactly at the end of the data vs one byte past; `0000:00:00` and 17 other malformed dates (month 13, Feb 30, 24:00:00, year 1989 and next year + 1, wrong separators, single-digit hour, non-ASCII bytes); the year window edges; offset tag present, absent and 7 malformed forms (date kept, offset dropped); 0x9004 fallback and precedence; duplicate entries (first wins); both byte orders; JPEG, PNG eXIf (before and after IDAT), PNG raw profile and HEIC carriers; XMP-only date is not read; orientation and date from the same EXIF; `load_oriented` pixel-identical to `load_image`; invalid TIFF header still `ImageRejected` in the load path and `(None, None)` from `read_taken_at`; garbage and oversize input to `read_taken_at`; 4,000 mutated blocks and 500 random bodies never raise and only ever return valid values (control: over 100 mutated blocks still yield a date); the IFD spy stays silent on JPEG, PNG eXIf and PNG raw-profile carriers (control: the same spy fires when Pillow parses the EXIF itself).
- Mutation checks on a scratch copy (`-B`, deleted afterwards; control run unmodified: 109 passed at the time): 22 mutations, 22 killed (no entry cap, sub-IFD pointer at IFD0 followed, IFD pointer into the header, value offset into the header, NUL unchecked, value type unchecked, value count unchecked, value bound unchecked, year floor, year ceiling, pointer type unchecked, pointer count unchecked, last duplicate wins, no digitized fallback, digitized preferred over original, loose offset text, loose date text, EXIF never read in `load_image`, HEIC branch dropped in `read_taken_at`, Pillow asked to parse EXIF, an exception escaping `read_taken_at`, TIFF magic unchecked). Six of these first survived (the header/IFD guards and the digitized preference were only reachable through unreadable bytes) and were killed by adding direct helper tests and a two-date layout; one first-run mutation also hit the orientation reader's twin line and was re-targeted at the new reader.

Real photos (`data/real_photos/real_01..05.jpg`, read in place, nothing copied): all five load as before, and none carries a DateTimeOriginal, DateTimeDigitized, OffsetTimeOriginal or IFD0 DateTime (checked with our reader and with Pillow's: all None), so the real-photo test only exercises the "no date" path; the date path is covered by the hand-built carriers above.

Not run: the full suite. After the B-66 work on `schemas.py` began, collection fails with `ImportError: cannot import name 'SessionCreate' from 'rga.web.schemas'` (coding-3's in-flight change), and `tests/integration/test_share_image.py` now errors at fixture setup for the same reason (its 60 tests passed before that change; it seeds through `seed_session`). Neither is caused by R3. R3's own files and the existing io suite do not import the web layer and are green.

**Incident found and fixed while running R9 (my own mistake):** 20 `python -c "while True: pass"` processes from my loaded smoke runs in T3.9 (PIDs 18629-18648) were never stopped: my cleanup used an unquoted `$HOGS`, which zsh does not split, so `kill -9` and the `kill -0` verification both silently did nothing and I reported "hogs left: 0". They ran for 13 h 50 min (ppid 1, identical etime 13:49:42) and probably explain much of the machine load (load averages 30-48 fell to about 5 within minutes of killing them). Killed by explicit PID, verified by PID. The T3.9 smoke result itself is unaffected; any timing measured under that load was.

**Residue after R9:** no hog, no orphaned `rga-live-*` server (ppid 1), no `rga-smoke.*` or `rga-upgrade-*` temp dir; logs and scratch trees removed.

## R8 follow-up (coding-2): check-then-act races in tests/e2e/pages.py

- Race found by R9: `confirm_reference` checked the URL, then read `#reference-app`'s `data-review-url`; a navigation between the two made the read wait 20 s. Replaced by `_settle_reference`, which looks at the page in one atomic `page.evaluate` (url, result card, warnings, continue link; retried when the read spans a navigation), follows the review link when the card offers one, and fails loudly after 30 s.
- The same pattern hunted through pages.py: `flash`, `group_correction_text`, `zeroing_advice` and the `#ag-next` read now use one atomic read (`visible_text` / `_read_page`); stale-result waits fixed with the "clear the old result, then wait" pattern in `analysis_aggregate` and `analysis_search` (a second press returned the previous result: it made `test_a_single_group_overlay_still_draws_one_color_and_one_legend_entry` fail once in a full run); `review_detect`, `review_save` and `review_fill` wait for their own API response. `/groups` (the Groups tab) is added to `NAV_PATHS`.
- `.venv/bin/pytest tests/e2e -q` -> `90 passed` on every one of 4 consecutive full runs (2 before and 2 after adding `/groups` to the nav sweep: 190 s, 185 s, 180 s, 156 s); ruff clean.

- Run: `.venv/bin/pytest tests/integration/test_api_photos.py tests/integration/test_api_review.py tests/unit/test_schemas.py tests/integration/test_api_client_contract.py tests/integration/test_id_bounds.py -q` -> exit 0. Together with `tests/unit/test_layering.py` and `tests/unit/test_templates_shell.py`: `841 passed`, including 3 Chromium tests. `ruff check src tests` is clean, and `ruff format --check` is clean on every file I touched. The 5 files it still lists are others': tests/synth/generate.py, tests/unit/test_synth.py, tests/smoke/smoke_roundtrip.py, tests/support/live_server.py, tests/unit/test_scripts_syntax.py.
- **Code:**
  - `services/photos.py`: `reference_work`, `reset_reference`, `ReferenceInUse` (409 `reference_in_use`).
  - `POST /api/photos/{id}/reference/reset`: no request body, so it follows the finalize and fill-shortfall pattern. The response is `ReferenceResetOut {photo, cleared_groups}`.
  - Review page state `reference_change {mode: direct|confirm|blocked, draft_groups, reason}`; None without a photo or with an unconfirmed reference.
  - `review.html` and `review.js`: the button above the shot counter, with an inline Continue/Cancel panel (no window.confirm).
  - design §4.7: one new row; the registry grows to 48.
- **What the tests check:**
  - **Service and route:**
    - reset clears the photo's reference and the shots, rejections, aim and region of its draft groups (counting only those with saved work);
    - other photos' groups and finals are untouched;
    - 409 when a final group is on the photo, with nothing changed;
    - rollback on an injected IntegrityError;
    - 404, 403 (foreign origin), 415 (text/plain body), 503 busy and a non-masked 500;
    - an unconfirmed photo resets harmlessly;
    - a different reference can be confirmed afterwards.
  - **Page state:** nothing saved or a region alone gives direct; saved shots or an aim on any draft group gives confirm with a count; a final group gives blocked with the reason. Other photos don't count, and there is no state without a photo or a confirmed reference.
  - **Chromium:** a confirm, reference page, re-confirm and back-to-review flow (return_to preserved, scale transform changed). It also covers the blocked state (disabled, with reason, click does nothing) and the direct state (navigates with no reset request).
  - **Contract and sweep:** the client contract requires the new call, the id sweep covers it, and the schema registry tests were updated.
- Mutation checks (scratch copy `/tmp/mut_u`, `python -B`, deleted afterwards; unmodified control green): 41 valid mutants of the service, route, page state, JS and template.
  - The first pass killed 39 of 41. One survivor was a malformed mutant of mine; it was replaced by two proper mutants ("reference not reset at all", "reference_kind not cleared"), both killed. The template-order mutant was replaced by "button moved below counter" and a label change, both killed.
  - One documented equivalent mutant: the drafts query including finals is unreachable, because `_any_final` raises first inside the same transaction.

- B-79 follow-up: the reference.html locked notice now points at "Change scale reference" in the review editor and notes a finalized group locks the scale; test_api_photos.py asserts the new text, and the negative assertions on the unlocked pages now check the absence of "Change scale reference". `pytest tests/integration/test_api_photos.py tests/unit/test_templates_shell.py -q` passed (exit 0).

## T4.0v share header lost the suppressor (B-81) (coding-6)
- Root cause: `services/share.py::_compose` drew the conditions line "date | distance | position | suppressor" as one line through `_fit`, which cuts text to the card width (920 px at 26 px type) and ends it with "...". With a named suppressor the line is too long for any realistic name: reproduced with a text spy, "2026-09-30 08:15 | 300 yd | kneeling sitting | Suppressed (Thunder Beast Ar..." (bench: "...Ultra 7..."), so the suppressor name, and with long position names the suppressor itself, were cut off the export. The data path was fine: every field is read fresh from the group; the PATCH carries suppressor and suppressor_id; the page reloads after Save so the card and preview are rebuilt; the route sends no-store.
- Fix (share.py, coding-5's file, minimal): new `_wrap_parts` joins the four condition parts with " | " onto as few lines as fit, wrapping at part boundaries and, for a part wider than a line, at its words; `_compose` draws those lines. `_header_lines` is unchanged, so the existing header tests still pass.
- Regression tests: `tests/e2e/test_share_context.py` (9 tests, Chromium against a live server: suppressor on, named, off, position, date, time and distance edits each change the share PNG header band; renaming the suppressor changes it again and the no-store image refetches; two long names that differ only at their ends draw different headers) and `TestShareHeaderKeepsTheConditions` in `tests/integration/test_pages_b66.py` (text spy: a 35 character suppressor name with each of bench, kneeling_sitting, standing_field is drawn in full with no "..."; a short header stays on one line; an API edit appears on the next image with no-store).
- Verifier checks: the "cut off" e2e test fails against an unfixed scratch copy of the repo (both names produce identical header bands); with `_wrap_parts` patched back to a single line the three truncation tests fail (3 failed, 2 passed). Scratch copy and plugin removed.
- `.venv/bin/pytest tests/integration/test_pages_b66.py tests/integration/test_share_image.py tests/e2e/test_share_context.py -q` -> 119 passed (64 s). ruff clean.
